People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I guess what finally scared me into coming is that I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I know that sounds awful to say out loud.

Sometimes after work, driving home from the clinic — it’s always worse after those long days, you know, with the animals and the clients and everything — I catch myself thinking, like… what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. Not that I want to talk dramatically, I just… it frightened me that the thought was even there.

Not that I want to talk dramatically, I just… it frightened me that the thought was even there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… the really intense part has been about a month. Yeah… yes, I have. It’s actually one of the things that scares me, because it’s not like me. I haven’t touched my camera at all, and usually I’d be taking pictures of the birds or the light in the yard or something ridiculous like a leaf with dew on it.

I guess… yes, but not exactly fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I suddenly got this idea that I had to fix everything, organize everything.

And then I’d sort of look around and realize everyone else was just… living their day, and I had turned it into this giant operation.

Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m limping or anything, it’s more like everything takes this huge amount of effort.

At work, just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. And it’s silly because I’ve done that walk a thousand times, you know? Um… strange thoughts like… I don’t think so. Not like believing something impossible, I mean.

And I know that sounds awful to say out loud.\n\nSometimes after work, driving home from the clinic — it’s always worse after those long days, you know, with the animals and the clients and everything — I catch myself thinking, like… what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. Not that I want to talk dramatically, I just… it frightened me that the thought was even there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… the really intense part has been about a month. Since all this upheaval around taking in our relative started. It’s hard to separate it, because there’s always been stress, and then work gets mixed in, and family things never stay in one box, you know? But the thoughts like that — the not wanting to wake up, the scary driving-home kind — that’s been about a month.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. It’s actually one of the things that scares me, because it’s not like me. I haven’t touched my camera at all, and usually I’d be taking pictures of the birds or the light in the yard or something ridiculous like a leaf with dew on it.\n\nAnd the bird feeders — I still see them there, obviously, and I’ll think, oh, I should check them, but there’s no… pull to it. Same with the garden. Normally I’m looking ahead to what’s coming up next, planning things, fussing over it too much probably. Now I just don’t really care, and those things used to be how I felt like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… my energy is awful. At work, even just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud.\n\nAnd it’s silly because it’s not far — I mean, we do that walk a hundred times in a day, and I used to not even think about it. Now I’m aware of every step, like my legs are heavy or I’m moving too slowly. Then by the time I get home, I’m just… done. I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening care routine. I’ll stand in the kitchen staring at something I know how to cook and just feel like I don’t have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… sleep has been a mess. I’ll get into bed and then my brain just starts replaying everything — every mistake I made that day, or honestly mistakes from years ago that have no business showing up at two in the morning.\n\nAnd then other nights I do fall asleep, but I’m awake before dawn, like my body just decides we’re done, except I’m exhausted. I’ll stay in bed longer sometimes, thinking maybe if I just lie there it’ll count somehow, but I don’t feel rested. I used to be able to run in the mornings, and now I can barely get myself functional enough to start the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. A lot, actually. I keep telling myself I’m a terrible person because I resent how disrupted everything feels at home, and then I feel guilty for even thinking that, because people are just needing what they need, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll replay every little thing — like if I gave an impatient look, or my voice got sharp, or I didn’t respond the way I should have. I’ll go over it again and again and it turns into this evidence pile in my head that I’m awful, that my husband and the family would be better off without me there making everything worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or do you feel like you're moving in slow motion?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but not exactly fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I suddenly got this idea that I had to fix everything, organize everything.\n\nI took extra shifts at work, and then at home I redid the whole care schedule — like, sat there making lists and moving things around — and I was calling contractors and trying to coordinate everyone’s tasks like it was some huge hospital project. Which is ridiculous because no one asked me to take that much on. I just felt like if I didn’t keep pushing and managing it all, it would all fall apart.\n\nAnd then I’d sort of look around and realize everyone else was just… living their day, and I had turned it into this giant operation.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. It’s like everything’s still happening around me — the house is noisy, people are coming and going, there’s laundry, dishes, all of that — but it feels like the color got drained out of everything.\n\nI’ll go into the laundry room sometimes and just cry there, because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing me like that. It feels… hopeless, I guess. Not like voices or seeing things that aren’t there.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll hear a noise in the house and think someone called me, but then it’s just the dishwasher or someone moving around, and this house has been so loud lately that it’s hard to tell what’s what. My husband says the pipes make this knocking sound too, which drives me crazy because then I’m checking rooms like I’m the only person who hears it, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that where I’m hearing voices or seeing things other people can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans for how you would harm yourself if you decided to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like… a written-out plan or anything. Not really… I mean, there might be moments where I can act normal, like if someone’s in the room or there’s something that has to get done. The house can be busy, people moving around, laundry going, all of that, and I’m technically there.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I… I guess what finally scared me into coming is that I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I know that sounds awful to say out loud.\n\nSometimes after work, driving home from the clinic — it’s always worse after those long days, you know, with the animals and the clients and everything — I catch myself thinking, like… what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. Not that I want to talk dramatically, I just… it frightened me that the thought was even there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… the really intense part has been about a month. Since all this upheaval around taking in our relative started. It’s hard to separate it, because there’s always been stress, and then work gets mixed in, and family things never stay in one box, you know? But the thoughts like that — the not wanting to wake up, the scary driving-home kind — that’s been about a month.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. It’s actually one of the things that scares me, because it’s not like me. I haven’t touched my camera at all, and usually I’d be taking pictures of the birds or the light in the yard or something ridiculous like a leaf with dew on it.\n\nAnd the bird feeders — I still see them there, obviously, and I’ll think, oh, I should check them, but there’s no… pull to it. Same with the garden. Normally I’m looking ahead to what’s coming up next, planning things, fussing over it too much probably. Now I just don’t really care, and those things used to be how I felt like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… my energy is awful. At work, even just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud.\n\nAnd it’s silly because it’s not far — I mean, we do that walk a hundred times in a day, and I used to not even think about it. Now I’m aware of every step, like my legs are heavy or I’m moving too slowly. Then by the time I get home, I’m just… done. I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening care routine. I’ll stand in the kitchen staring at something I know how to cook and just feel like I don’t have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… sleep has been a mess. I’ll get into bed and then my brain just starts replaying everything — every mistake I made that day, or honestly mistakes from years ago that have no business showing up at two in the morning.\n\nAnd then other nights I do fall asleep, but I’m awake before dawn, like my body just decides we’re done, except I’m exhausted. I’ll stay in bed longer sometimes, thinking maybe if I just lie there it’ll count somehow, but I don’t feel rested. I used to be able to run in the mornings, and now I can barely get myself functional enough to start the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. A lot, actually. I keep telling myself I’m a terrible person because I resent how disrupted everything feels at home, and then I feel guilty for even thinking that, because people are just needing what they need, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll replay every little thing — like if I gave an impatient look, or my voice got sharp, or I didn’t respond the way I should have. I’ll go over it again and again and it turns into this evidence pile in my head that I’m awful, that my husband and the family would be better off without me there making everything worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or do you feel like you're moving in slow motion?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but not exactly fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I suddenly got this idea that I had to fix everything, organize everything.\n\nI took extra shifts at work, and then at home I redid the whole care schedule — like, sat there making lists and moving things around — and I was calling contractors and trying to coordinate everyone’s tasks like it was some huge hospital project. Which is ridiculous because no one asked me to take that much on. I just felt like if I didn’t keep pushing and managing it all, it would all fall apart.\n\nAnd then I’d sort of look around and realize everyone else was just… living their day, and I had turned it into this giant operation.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. It’s like everything’s still happening around me — the house is noisy, people are coming and going, there’s laundry, dishes, all of that — but it feels like the color got drained out of everything.\n\nI’ll go into the laundry room sometimes and just cry there, because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing me like that. It feels… hopeless, I guess. Like I’m there, but I’m not really in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual perceptions, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like voices or seeing things that aren’t there.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll hear a noise in the house and think someone called me, but then it’s just the dishwasher or someone moving around, and this house has been so loud lately that it’s hard to tell what’s what. My husband says the pipes make this knocking sound too, which drives me crazy because then I’m checking rooms like I’m the only person who hears it, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that where I’m hearing voices or seeing things other people can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans for how you would harm yourself if you decided to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like… a written-out plan or anything. But what scared me enough to come in is that I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd then sometimes driving home from the clinic — it’s usually after a long shift, and the road just feels endless — I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. Then I get home and I’m feeding everyone or putting things away like nothing happened, which makes it feel even stranger.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly about the number on the scale… I’ve kind of been avoiding checking, honestly.\n\nMy eating’s been weird though. Some days I just have coffee and maybe pick at toast, and then I’ll get through a whole clinic shift and realize I barely ate anything. Then I’m surprised I feel shaky, like somehow I forgot food is a thing. I keep meaning to pack something decent, but then the morning gets away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than usual or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m limping or anything, it’s more like everything takes this huge amount of effort.\n\nAt work, just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. And it’s silly because I’ve done that walk a thousand times, you know? There’s always someone asking where the slides are or the centrifuge beeping, and I’ll start thinking about whether we’re out of gloves in the other room—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nBut yes. By the time I get home, I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening routine. It feels like my body is just… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like beliefs that aren't based in reality, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um… strange thoughts like… I don’t think so. Not like believing something impossible, I mean.\n\nSometimes I get in my head about things and then I’ll kind of spiral—like at work if something is missing, I’ll think, “Did I put that somewhere ridiculous?” and then I’m retracing everything I did, and then I’m thinking about the supply order and whether I forgot to tell someone about the gloves, and then I’m not even sure what I was originally looking for.\n\nBut that’s probably not what you mean. I don’t think I’ve had beliefs that other people would say aren’t real. At least… not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I kind of said this already, but yes. It really does feel slower, or heavier maybe.\n\nLike at the clinic, just getting from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. And then I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that thing while I’m over here,” and somehow even that feels like too much. By the time I get home, I’m just wiped out. Making dinner or helping with the evening care routine feels almost impossible some nights.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of elevated mood or increased energy over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… earlier this month there was a stretch where I felt almost, I don’t know, electrically upbeat? Like my mood was way up and I had this burst of energy.\n\nBut it wasn’t just pleasant. I was also really sharp with people. I snapped over tiny things—like a medication cup being misplaced or someone changing the morning schedule at work. And normally that stuff annoys me maybe, but I don’t usually react like that. It felt like I wasn’t quite myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days over the past month where you felt relatively normal, without these depressed feelings?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, there might be moments where I can act normal, like if someone’s in the room or there’s something that has to get done. The house can be busy, people moving around, laundry going, all of that, and I’m technically there.\n\nBut most days it feels like the color has just drained out of everything. I’ll go into the laundry room and cry because I can’t stand anyone seeing how hopeless I feel. And then I’ll come back out and try to fold towels or answer a question like nothing happened. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had many days that felt normal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… kind of. Not like I think the TV is sending me messages or anything like that. It’s more… I get really convinced that I’m just a terrible person.

Like with all the disruption at home, I’ll feel resentful for even a second, and then I’ll spiral into, “What kind of person thinks that?” And I’ll replay every impatient look I gave someone, every sharp comment, even tiny things I probably shouldn’t make so big. I’ll be washing a mug or standing by the dryer and suddenly I’m going over it again like it’s evidence.

And then it starts to feel completely true that my husband and the family would be better off without me around. Part of me knows that’s probably… not fair, or maybe not totally true. But in the moment it feels real.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I… I guess what finally scared me into coming is that I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And I know that sounds awful to say out loud.\n\nSometimes after work, driving home from the clinic — it’s always worse after those long days, you know, with the animals and the clients and everything — I catch myself thinking, like… what would happen if I just didn’t turn the wheel. Not that I want to talk dramatically, I just… it frightened me that the thought was even there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… the really intense part has been about a month. Since all this upheaval around taking in our relative started. But the thoughts like that — the not wanting to wake up, the scary driving-home kind — that’s been about a month.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. It’s actually one of the things that scares me, because it’s not like me. I haven’t touched my camera at all, and usually I’d be taking pictures of the birds or the light in the yard or something ridiculous like a leaf with dew on it.\n\nAnd the bird feeders — I still see them there, obviously, and I’ll think, oh, I should check them, but there’s no… pull to it. Same with the garden. Normally I’m looking ahead to what’s coming up next, planning things, fussing over it too much probably. Now I just don’t really care, and those things used to be how I felt like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… my energy is awful. At work, even just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud.\n\nAnd it’s silly because it’s not far — I mean, we do that walk a hundred times in a day, and I used to not even think about it. Now I’m aware of every step, like my legs are heavy or I’m moving too slowly. Then by the time I get home, I’m just… done. I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening care routine. I’ll stand in the kitchen staring at something I know how to cook and just feel like I don’t have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… sleep has been a mess. I’ll get into bed and then my brain just starts replaying everything — every mistake I made that day, or honestly mistakes from years ago that have no business showing up at two in the morning.\n\nAnd then other nights I do fall asleep, but I’m awake before dawn, like my body just decides we’re done, except I’m exhausted. I’ll stay in bed longer sometimes, thinking maybe if I just lie there it’ll count somehow, but I don’t feel rested. I used to be able to run in the mornings, and now I can barely get myself functional enough to start the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. A lot, actually. I keep telling myself I’m a terrible person because I resent how disrupted everything feels at home, and then I feel guilty for even thinking that, because people are just needing what they need, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll replay every little thing — like if I gave an impatient look, or my voice got sharp, or I didn’t respond the way I should have. I’ll go over it again and again and it turns into this evidence pile in my head that I’m awful, that my husband and the family would be better off without me there making everything worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or do you feel like you're moving in slow motion?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but not exactly fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I suddenly got this idea that I had to fix everything, organize everything.\n\nI took extra shifts at work, and then at home I redid the whole care schedule — like, sat there making lists and moving things around — and I was calling contractors and trying to coordinate everyone’s tasks like it was some huge hospital project. Which is ridiculous because no one asked me to take that much on. I just felt like if I didn’t keep pushing and managing it all, it would all fall apart.\n\nAnd then I’d sort of look around and realize everyone else was just… living their day, and I had turned it into this giant operation.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. It’s like everything’s still happening around me — the house is noisy, people are coming and going, there’s laundry, dishes, all of that — but it feels like the color got drained out of everything.\n\nI’ll go into the laundry room sometimes and just cry there, because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing me like that. It feels… hopeless, I guess. Like I’m there, but I’m not really in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual perceptions, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like voices or seeing things that aren’t there.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll hear a noise in the house and think someone called me, but then it’s just the dishwasher or someone moving around, and this house has been so loud lately that it’s hard to tell what’s what. My husband says the pipes make this knocking sound too, which drives me crazy because then I’m checking rooms like I’m the only person who hears it, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that where I’m hearing voices or seeing things other people can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans for how you would harm yourself if you decided to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like… a written-out plan or anything. But what scared me enough to come in is that I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd then sometimes driving home from the clinic — it’s usually after a long shift, and the road just feels endless — I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. Then I get home and I’m feeding everyone or putting things away like nothing happened, which makes it feel even stranger.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly about the number on the scale… I’ve kind of been avoiding checking, honestly.\n\nMy eating’s been weird though. Some days I just have coffee and maybe pick at toast, and then I’ll get through a whole clinic shift and realize I barely ate anything. Then I’m surprised I feel shaky, like somehow I forgot food is a thing. I keep meaning to pack something decent, but then the morning gets away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than usual or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. And it’s silly because I’ve done that walk a thousand times, you know? There’s always someone asking where the slides are or the centrifuge beeping, and I’ll start thinking about whether we’re out of gloves in the other room—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nBut yes. By the time I get home, I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening routine. It feels like my body is just… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like beliefs that aren't based in reality, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um… strange thoughts like… I don’t think so. Yes. There have been days where I just… I couldn’t keep up with normal life.

At the clinic I’ve started making mistakes, which scares me because, you know, there isn’t a lot of room for being careless with animals or medications or charts. And then I start thinking everyone notices, and I try to compensate, and that almost makes me worse.

I’ve been avoiding friends too. Not because I don’t care about them—I do—but I’m afraid I’ll either snap at somebody over nothing or I’ll just fall apart in front of them. So I don’t answer messages, or I tell myself I’ll call back later and then I don’t.

And at home… my husband has had to take over a lot. Bills, meals, just basic things I usually handle. Sometimes even just keeping an eye on me when my thoughts get dark. I hate saying that out loud, but yeah, there have been periods this month where I really wasn’t functioning.

I’ll get into bed and then my brain just starts replaying everything — every mistake I made that day, or honestly mistakes from years ago that have no business showing up at two in the morning.\n\nAnd then other nights I do fall asleep, but I’m awake before dawn, like my body just decides we’re done, except I’m exhausted. I’ll stay in bed longer sometimes, thinking maybe if I just lie there it’ll count somehow, but I don’t feel rested. I used to be able to run in the mornings, and now I can barely get myself functional enough to start the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I’ll go over it again and again and it turns into this evidence pile in my head that I’m awful, that my husband and the family would be better off without me there making everything worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or do you feel like you're moving in slow motion?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but not exactly fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I suddenly got this idea that I had to fix everything, organize everything.\n\nI took extra shifts at work, and then at home I redid the whole care schedule — like, sat there making lists and moving things around — and I was calling contractors and trying to coordinate everyone’s tasks like it was some huge hospital project. Which is ridiculous because no one asked me to take that much on. I just felt like if I didn’t keep pushing and managing it all, it would all fall apart.\n\nAnd then I’d sort of look around and realize everyone else was just… living their day, and I had turned it into this giant operation.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. It’s like everything’s still happening around me — the house is noisy, people are coming and going, there’s laundry, dishes, all of that — but it feels like the color got drained out of everything.\n\nI’ll go into the laundry room sometimes and just cry there, because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing me like that. It feels… hopeless, I guess. Not like voices or seeing things that aren’t there.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll hear a noise in the house and think someone called me, but then it’s just the dishwasher or someone moving around, and this house has been so loud lately that it’s hard to tell what’s what. My husband says the pipes make this knocking sound too, which drives me crazy because then I’m checking rooms like I’m the only person who hears it, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that where I’m hearing voices or seeing things other people can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans for how you would harm yourself if you decided to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like… a written-out plan or anything. Then I get home and I’m feeding everyone or putting things away like nothing happened, which makes it feel even stranger.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly about the number on the scale… I’ve kind of been avoiding checking, honestly.\n\nMy eating’s been weird though. Then I’m surprised I feel shaky, like somehow I forgot food is a thing. I keep meaning to pack something decent, but then the morning gets away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than usual or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m limping or anything, it’s more like everything takes this huge amount of effort.\n\nAt work, just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. Some mornings, yes, it feels like that. I mean, I do get up eventually most of the time because the house is moving and there’s noise and people need things, and then I start thinking about laundry or towels or whatever’s sitting there.

It’s hard to separate it, because there’s always been stress, and then work gets mixed in, and family things never stay in one box, you know? But the thoughts like that — the not wanting to wake up, the scary driving-home kind — that’s been about a month.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I have. It’s actually one of the things that scares me, because it’s not like me. I haven’t touched my camera at all, and usually I’d be taking pictures of the birds or the light in the yard or something ridiculous like a leaf with dew on it.\n\nAnd the bird feeders — I still see them there, obviously, and I’ll think, oh, I should check them, but there’s no… pull to it. Same with the garden. Normally I’m looking ahead to what’s coming up next, planning things, fussing over it too much probably. Now I just don’t really care, and those things used to be how I felt like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you're moving throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… my energy is awful. At work, even just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud.\n\nAnd it’s silly because it’s not far — I mean, we do that walk a hundred times in a day, and I used to not even think about it. Now I’m aware of every step, like my legs are heavy or I’m moving too slowly. Then by the time I get home, I’m just… done. I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening care routine. I’ll stand in the kitchen staring at something I know how to cook and just feel like I don’t have it in me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… sleep has been a mess. I’ll get into bed and then my brain just starts replaying everything — every mistake I made that day, or honestly mistakes from years ago that have no business showing up at two in the morning.\n\nAnd then other nights I do fall asleep, but I’m awake before dawn, like my body just decides we’re done, except I’m exhausted. I’ll stay in bed longer sometimes, thinking maybe if I just lie there it’ll count somehow, but I don’t feel rested. I used to be able to run in the mornings, and now I can barely get myself functional enough to start the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. A lot, actually. I keep telling myself I’m a terrible person because I resent how disrupted everything feels at home, and then I feel guilty for even thinking that, because people are just needing what they need, you know?\n\nAnd then I’ll replay every little thing — like if I gave an impatient look, or my voice got sharp, or I didn’t respond the way I should have. I’ll go over it again and again and it turns into this evidence pile in my head that I’m awful, that my husband and the family would be better off without me there making everything worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or do you feel like you're moving in slow motion?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but not exactly fidgety like tapping my foot. It’s more like I suddenly got this idea that I had to fix everything, organize everything.\n\nI took extra shifts at work, and then at home I redid the whole care schedule — like, sat there making lists and moving things around — and I was calling contractors and trying to coordinate everyone’s tasks like it was some huge hospital project. Which is ridiculous because no one asked me to take that much on. I just felt like if I didn’t keep pushing and managing it all, it would all fall apart.\n\nAnd then I’d sort of look around and realize everyone else was just… living their day, and I had turned it into this giant operation.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. It’s like everything’s still happening around me — the house is noisy, people are coming and going, there’s laundry, dishes, all of that — but it feels like the color got drained out of everything.\n\nI’ll go into the laundry room sometimes and just cry there, because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing me like that. It feels… hopeless, I guess. Like I’m there, but I’m not really in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual perceptions, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like voices or seeing things that aren’t there.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll hear a noise in the house and think someone called me, but then it’s just the dishwasher or someone moving around, and this house has been so loud lately that it’s hard to tell what’s what. My husband says the pipes make this knocking sound too, which drives me crazy because then I’m checking rooms like I’m the only person who hears it, but… no, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that where I’m hearing voices or seeing things other people can’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans for how you would harm yourself if you decided to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I don’t have, like… a written-out plan or anything. But what scared me enough to come in is that I keep thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd then sometimes driving home from the clinic — it’s usually after a long shift, and the road just feels endless — I catch myself wondering what would happen if I just… didn’t turn the wheel. Then I get home and I’m feeding everyone or putting things away like nothing happened, which makes it feel even stranger.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly about the number on the scale… I’ve kind of been avoiding checking, honestly.\n\nMy eating’s been weird though. Some days I just have coffee and maybe pick at toast, and then I’ll get through a whole clinic shift and realize I barely ate anything. Then I’m surprised I feel shaky, like somehow I forgot food is a thing. I keep meaning to pack something decent, but then the morning gets away from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than usual or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m limping or anything, it’s more like everything takes this huge amount of effort.\n\nAt work, just walking from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. And it’s silly because I’ve done that walk a thousand times, you know? There’s always someone asking where the slides are or the centrifuge beeping, and I’ll start thinking about whether we’re out of gloves in the other room—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nBut yes. By the time I get home, I can barely get dinner together or help with the evening routine. It feels like my body is just… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like beliefs that aren't based in reality, over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um… strange thoughts like… I don’t think so. Not like believing something impossible, I mean.\n\nSometimes I get in my head about things and then I’ll kind of spiral—like at work if something is missing, I’ll think, “Did I put that somewhere ridiculous?” and then I’m retracing everything I did, and then I’m thinking about the supply order and whether I forgot to tell someone about the gloves, and then I’m not even sure what I was originally looking for.\n\nBut that’s probably not what you mean. I don’t think I’ve had beliefs that other people would say aren’t real. At least… not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I kind of said this already, but yes. It really does feel slower, or heavier maybe.\n\nLike at the clinic, just getting from the treatment area over to the lab benches feels like I’m dragging myself through mud. And then I’ll think, “Oh, I need to grab that thing while I’m over here,” and somehow even that feels like too much. By the time I get home, I’m just wiped out. Making dinner or helping with the evening care routine feels almost impossible some nights.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of elevated mood or increased energy over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… earlier this month there was a stretch where I felt almost, I don’t know, electrically upbeat? Like my mood was way up and I had this burst of energy.\n\nBut it wasn’t just pleasant. I was also really sharp with people. I snapped over tiny things—like a medication cup being misplaced or someone changing the morning schedule at work. And normally that stuff annoys me maybe, but I don’t usually react like that. It felt like I wasn’t quite myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days over the past month where you felt relatively normal, without these depressed feelings?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, there might be moments where I can act normal, like if someone’s in the room or there’s something that has to get done. The house can be busy, people moving around, laundry going, all of that, and I’m technically there.\n\nBut most days it feels like the color has just drained out of everything. I’ll go into the laundry room and cry because I can’t stand anyone seeing how hopeless I feel. And then I’ll come back out and try to fold towels or answer a question like nothing happened. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had many days that felt normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that you think might not be true, but seem real to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. Not like I think the TV is sending me messages or anything like that. It’s more… I get really convinced that I’m just a terrible person.\n\nLike with all the disruption at home, I’ll feel resentful for even a second, and then I’ll spiral into, “What kind of person thinks that?” And I’ll replay every impatient look I gave someone, every sharp comment, even tiny things I probably shouldn’t make so big. I’ll be washing a mug or standing by the dryer and suddenly I’m going over it again like it’s evidence.\n\nAnd then it starts to feel completely true that my husband and the family would be better off without me around. Part of me knows that’s probably… not fair, or maybe not totally true. But in the moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past month where your depressive symptoms were so severe that you were unable to carry out your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been days where I just… I couldn’t keep up with normal life.\n\nAt the clinic I’ve started making mistakes, which scares me because, you know, there isn’t a lot of room for being careless with animals or medications or charts. And then I start thinking everyone notices, and I try to compensate, and that almost makes me worse.\n\nI’ve been avoiding friends too. Not because I don’t care about them—I do—but I’m afraid I’ll either snap at somebody over nothing or I’ll just fall apart in front of them. So I don’t answer messages, or I tell myself I’ll call back later and then I don’t.\n\nAnd at home… my husband has had to take over a lot. Bills, meals, just basic things I usually handle. Sometimes even just keeping an eye on me when my thoughts get dark. I hate saying that out loud, but yeah, there have been periods this month where I really wasn’t functioning.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days over the past month where you felt so low that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: Some mornings, yes, it feels like that. I mean, I do get up eventually most of the time because the house is moving and there’s noise and people need things, and then I start thinking about laundry or towels or whatever’s sitting there.\n\nBut inside, it’s like everything has gone flat. Like the color is drained out of the day before I’ve even started. I’ll end up in the laundry room crying because I can’t stand the idea of anyone seeing how hopeless I feel. So… maybe I’m not always literally in bed all day, but I feel that low most days.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
